Assignment 1 (ungraded)
Civil Procedure 2 & 7, 2020
Professor Jim Greiner

## Logistics and Rules

Posting date: September 25, 2020
Due date/time: 9 am on Tuesday, September 29, 2020
Word limit: 825 words
Time limit: 2.5 hours

1. Prepare for the assignment however you'd like. I suggest reviewing and outlining the course materials through (and including) the posting date, nothing more.
2. Download the substantive assignment from the course website.
3. The word limit is listed above. Compose a response to the assignment in this number of words or less, including footnotes (if any), case citations (if any), etc. If your response is longer than the word limit, the first set of words up to the word limit will be reviewed and the remainder ignored.
4. The time limit for this assignment is listed above. You must complete AND UPLOAD your response within the time limit, with the clock starting to tick as of when you download the assignment. Students are on their honor to comply with this time limit.
5. As suggested by #4, the only acceptable method of response for this assignment is to upload your typed response into the drop box created on the course website. Please notify me before the assignment is due if you anticipate any difficulty complying with this method of response.
6. You will not need to do any additional research or read any source materials other than those assigned for class. You will not receive additional credit for citing or relying on materials other than those assigned for class.
7. You may refer to or use any non-human source to aid in your answer. You may not, however, communicate with any person other than Professor Greiner regarding this assignment until its due date/time, listed above. This prohibition includes librarians.
8. Although I will read questions regarding the assignment submitted via email, it is unlikely that I will chose to answer them. If I do answer them, I will forward the question and answer to the entire class. The assignment is straightforward; don't make it more complicated than it is.
9. Your response is due on the due date/time above. Absolutely no excuse will be accepted for a late paper. This unavailability of excuses includes computer problems, travel (including the T) issues, alarm clocks, troubles with the HLS uploading system, etc. I suggest doing and handing in the assignment no later than the day before its due date to avoid any timeliness issue.
10. This assignment is ungraded.
11. PLEASE DO NOT PUT YOUR NAME ON YOUR RESPONSE. Please put ONLY your 8-digit Harvard ID number on your paper.

### Suggestions for your answer

- DON'T use memo style format (i.e., "TO" and "FROM," etc.). Just write your answer.
- Quote statutes and cases VERY rarely, if at all. We know what they say, and it takes up time and space.
- Cite cases rarely, and only when doing so might save you time. For example, you might say, "Minimum contacts, *International Shoe*," instead of a lengthy quotation of the standard.

---

<!-- page: 2 -->

## Substantive Assignment

Donkey is a businessman. He was born in New Hampshire and has lived there his entire life. He started a gum business when he was 17, quit school, and made the business his life's passion. Throughout the events relevant to this assignment, Donkey is registered to vote in New Hampshire and pays taxes there, and he runs his gum business out of a building built on his personal property in rural New Hampshire. He never incorporated the business. Donkey's gum business depends on purchases of raw materials from all over the United States. Donkey orders these materials online. Regarding sales, Donkey operates a sell-by-Internet business model. Customers visit www.DonkeyBestBlowingGum.com, create personal accounts in which they provide their addresses but not their financial information, and order their gum, using PayPal or a similar payment service. Donkey ships to the address provided by the customer, in the volume requested by the customer. By volume of sales, approximately 25% of Donkey's products are shipped to Massachusetts, 20% to New York, 20% to California, 10% each to Florida and Texas. The rest is sprinkled around various of the United States. Donkey buys no advertisements, and he maintains his website personally. Donkey maintains no salesforce, and he solicits no business. He depends on his website to sell gum.

Shrek was born in Rhode Island and lived there through high school, until he went to college at UMass Boston. When he graduated from college, he attended Harvard Business School, and decided he liked Massachusetts. At age 25, upon graduating from business school, Shrek used money he borrowed from his parents to start his investing business. At first, he lived at home in Rhode Island with his parents, with whom he has always stayed close. But over the next 18 months, as his business became more and more successful, he bought a luxury apartment in downtown Boston, where he set up his business office and conducts his meetings. Currently, he sleeps in the Boston apartment on weekdays, and returns home on the weekends to his parents' house. He has always been registered to vote in Rhode Island, but he started paying Massachusetts income taxes as soon as he began earning money, an arrangement he has continued for the past 15 years. Shrek buys and invests in businesses all over the world. Most of the businesses he invests in are incorporated in Delaware, but their principal places of business are located in more than 30 states and 15 foreign nations. He uses bank and investment accounts from Boston-based financial institutions so that he can use public transportation or app-rides to visit their locations (Shrek never learned to drive). Shrek's net worth is approximately $150,000,000.

MM Corporation, Inc. is a Massachusetts corporation with its principal place of business in Boston, Massachusetts. Its sole owner and employee is Mulan. MM's business model involves monetizing the cult of Mulan's personality. MM runs a website, a Facebook page, an Instagram account, a Twitter feed, and various other social media accounts and outlets. Mulan uses these platforms to post photographs she takes; short videos that she makes of her encounters with people she meets on the street, often while she is in disguise; and other short videos in which Mulan provides her opinion on current events and the quality of various products available on the market. All of the photographs and videos Mulan makes feature recognizable landmarks in Boston or elsewhere in Massachusetts, such as the Boston Common, the Public Garden in downtown Boston, beaches at Martha's Vineyard (a Massachusetts island), Boston's Bunker Hill, Boston's Museum of Fine Arts, and the Berkshire Mountains (in western Massachusetts).

<!-- page: 3 -->

MM generates revenue by selling space for advertisements on its social media accounts. Those ads are exclusively for products that Mulan endorses on its social media accounts. MM never accepts an ad solicitation until Mulan has first tested/used the product and decided whether she likes it. Because it sells no products of its own, MM depends entirely on the Internet traffic it drives to its social media accounts. As such, its reputation is essential to its business.

A key component of MM's business model is the public image and reputation that neither MM, nor Mulan, nor anyone else associated with MM ever accepts money to buy Mulan's endorsement. A banner on MM's website and its Facebook page say, "MM Is Your Independent Voice. Jeff Bezos Doesn't Have Enough Money To Buy Us." MM endorses only products Mulan buys in Massachusetts (where she can easily obtain them), although those products may also be sold elsewhere. When MM endorses a product, Mulan posts videos of herself consuming or otherwise using it at a recognizable Massachusetts location, such as the various Massachusetts landmarks identified above.

One of the products MM endorsed in the fall of 2018 was Donkey's Best Blowing Gum. In November of 2019, Mulan posted a video of herself, in disguise, handing out free samples of Donkey's gum to kids on the Boston Common, then interviewing those kids to ask them what they thought. Donkey's gum was popular with kids aged 8–12. The video concluded with Mulan unmasking herself, to the delight of the children who had gathered for the free gum samples, and saying, "As you can see, Massachusetts loves you, Donkey's Best Blowing Gum. We give you five bubbles out of five!"

In December of 2019, Mulan received an email from Shrek. In the email, Shrek offered to buy all of the stock in MM, Inc., and asked Mulan to name a price to begin negotiations. Intrigued, Mulan sought bids from other investment firms to see what the best offer might be. The highest offer she received was for $5,000,000. Mulan emailed Shrek an offer to begin negotiations based on a minimum price of five million dollars. Shrek returned an email offering to begin negotiations with a maximum price of $4,300,000. Mulan replied with an email refusing the offer and terminating the negotiation. Shrek ended the exchange with an email dated January 2, 2020, saying, "You should have taken my offer, Mulan. Very shortly, your company will be worth less than my maximum offer. I'll make sure of that."

Around February 2 of 2020, Mulan received an email from a friend with a link to a YouTube video. The video was a short clip, about five minutes in length. It showed what purported to be a conversation in a conference room at the New Hampshire Donkey estate between Mulan and Donkey. In the conference room conversation, Mulan purportedly proposed that Donkey pay Mulan $50,000 in exchange for Mulan's endorsement of Donkey's Best Blowing Gum on "all of MM's social media accounts."

In fact, Mulan had never set foot on the Donkey estate. Mulan had never communicated in any way with Donkey. Mulan had never met Donkey in person, and had never been to the Donkey headquarters. This video was a "deepfake," a highly realistic fake video.

Mulan hired a private investigator. The investigator interviewed one Elsa Anna, who until just a few days earlier had worked for Donkey in the gum manufacturing business. She recalled that one day in early January of 2020, while she had been at the Donkey New Hampshire property, Donkey had called her in to record a five-minute video clip featuring a conversation between Donkey and Shrek (whom she recognized from photos in financial magazines). Anna recalled that the conversation centered on the endorsement of Donkey's gum products, and that at the end, Shrek promised to endorse Donkey's gum on "all of MM's social media accounts" if Donkey would pay Shrek $50,000. At the time, Anna did not

<!-- page: 4 -->

know who "MM" was. Mulan believes that it is likely that someone (she thinks Shrek, or Donkey, or both) edited the video to insert Mulan into this video in Shrek's place.

Beginning in March of 2020, MM's advertisement revenue dropped. During the summer of 2020, Mulan received five offers to buy all of the stock in MM. The highest of those offers was $3,900,000. In the middle of August 2020, Mulan received an email from Shrek, once again offering to buy all of the stock of MM. In this email, Shrek stated that his best, highest, and final offer was $4,000,000.

Massachusetts is a Second Restatement state for choice of law. New Hampshire is a First Restatement state. Both states recently amended their long-arm statutes so as to allow their courts to exercise jurisdiction whenever the U.S. Constitution would so permit.

The law in all fifty of the United States includes the tort of "Business Disparagement." Under this tort, a defendant is liable to a plaintiff if the defendant participates in the production of a false or misleading video that harms the reputation, public perception, or standing of a business in a way that causes a loss of value to the business. The last event necessary to complete the Business Disparagement tort is the infliction of the harm, in the form of the loss of value by the business. But: Massachusetts, unlike any other state in the United States, provides an affirmative defense to the Business Disparagement tort called the "Public Access" defense. Under Massachusetts law, the Public Access defense means that if a defendant proves that the affected business has constant and ready access to the public or to media outlets, then the plaintiff cannot recover.

## Assignment Question

You are an associate in the law firm representing MM. The partner you work for tells you that she's thinking of recommending that MM sue Donkey and Shrek for $500,000 each in either Massachusetts or New Hampshire federal district court. She asks you for a memo analyzing whether either or both of those two federal district courts will dismiss against either or both defendants for lack of subject matter or personal jurisdiction. She also asks you whether the Massachusetts Public Access defense will apply to the lawsuit, assuming that the Massachusetts federal court applies Massachusetts choice of law rules and the New Hampshire federal court applies New Hampshire choice of law rules.

## 2020-assignment-01 · Exemplary student answer 1

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-01-altabet-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

## SMJ

**AUJ:** There's no FQJ because the well-pleaded complaint states a state, not federal, cause of action (*Mottley*). There is no *Smith-Grable* FQJ because there's no federal issue.

**DJ:** The complaint demands greater than $75k so AIC met for both.

MM is incorporated/PPOB in MA.

Donkey is a citizen of NH. Complete diversity between MM and Donkey.

Shrek's citizenship is unclear: RI or MA?

Shrek started as a RI citizen. He went to MA for schooling (definite end time, no change). Then he moved back in RI (still RI). He bought an apartment in MA, conducted business, paid taxes, and stayed on weekdays. He is in RI on weekends and registered to vote there. It's a close call if Shrek has an intent to remain indefinitely in MA (*Mas*). Given the 15-year pattern, I'd say he changed to MA. If true, there is no complete diversity and the court should dismiss the claim against Shrek. If Shrek has RI citizenship then there is complete diversity.

## PJ

**MA Court:**

There is no general jurisdiction over Donkey. If we analogize *Daimler*'s "at home" given Donkey's business activities, there is still none because 25% is not enough for continuous systemic contacts. If Shrek is domiciled in MA then there would be general jurisdiction over him, but that would mean no SMJ. If Shrek is domiciled in RI, then using MA banks is likely not enough for general jurisdiction.

There is unlikely to be specific jurisdiction over Donkey, but there should be over Shrek. Both states' long-arms go to limit of constitution and 4(k)(1)(a) allows the federal courts to piggyback. Regarding minimal contacts, Donkey did reach into MA to harm a MA-based business. He did not enter MA. The video posting is directing activity at the internet, not MA, even if he knows MM operates in that state. It's possible that MA might be center of Mulan's reputation (always filming at MA landmarks, *Calder*, Hollywood reputation). Fairness and reasonableness suggest that Donkey might be able to be brought in. He sells a significant portion of his goods to MA customers.

There's a much stronger case for bringing Shrek in since, in addition to targeting a MA-based business, he sent emails and might have been in MA when doing so. He also has constant business contacts with MA. Fairness and reasonableness should be met. MA also has a significant interest in protecting the reputation of resident corporations. It might be unfair for Donkey given lack of contact with the state, but Shrek has frequent contact. Convenience and fairness, however, does seem to posit NH as best since the relevant evidence should be there, and

<!-- page: 2 -->

a video can be viewed anywhere, although we don't know if Shrek doctored/uploaded the video in his MA apartment.

**NH Court:**

The NH court has general jurisdiction over Donkey (domiciled there) but not over Shrek (only one contact there, the making of the video).

The NH court might exercise specific jurisdiction over Shrek. See above regarding the long-arms, so we need minimum contacts. Minimal contacts relate defendant, forum and subject matter. Here the deepfake video appears to have been filmed in NH, linking the subject matter of the case, NH, and Shrek. Fairness and reasonableness might counsel for NH since relevant evidence should be there (such as Anna's testimony), and NH has an interest in regulating possibly illegal conduct occurring in its borders. But, other than going to NH for the filming, there appears to be no connection between Shrek and NH. This is possibly enough to allow for specific jurisdiction.

## HCOL

The public access defense is substantive (could affect pre-litigation conduct). HCOL analysis is necessary.

**NH Court:**

NH is First Restatement and will apply the law of where the last act needed to complete the harm occurred (LLD). The tort is complete when the loss of value occurs. MM's business is centered in MA and MA should be the location of damage. The affirmative defense will be available.

**MA Court:**

MA is 2R. LLD is the presumption, but check § 6 for whether other factors counsel override. The center of gravity is mixed. The recording was in NH, one participant's domicile was in NH, and the video was recorded in NH. However, the video may have been doctored in MA, the damage was there, and the scheme began with email sent to (and likely from) MA. NH has an interest to prevent deepfakes from being recorded there. But MA has an interest in defending resident corporations against falsehoods (especially one associated with MA landmarks) and the attack was made by a MA resident. At minimum, analysis is mixed and might even favor MA. MA law should apply under 2R and affirmative defense will be available.

## 2020-assignment-01 · Exemplary student answer 2

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-01-mccarthy-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

All-in-all, the MA court could dismiss for lack SMJ if Shrek is deemed a MA domiciliary, or for lack of IPJ against Donkey. The NH court might dismiss for IPJ against Shrek.

## SMJ

Business Disparagement is state law COA, with no federal issue, so there is no FQJ (*Mottley* + § 1331).

MM is incorporated in MA with MA PPOB. Donkey is domiciled in NH — he has lived there his whole life.

Shrek is domiciled in either MA or RI. Shrek was domiciled in RI, and he continues to visit his parents on the weekend and votes there. But Shrek has slept in MA during the week for many years, has business there, pays taxes there, and intends to continue doing all this, so his domicile may have changed to MA (*Mas*).

If the court determines Shrek is an MA domiciliary, it will dismiss for lack of complete diversity (*Strawbridge*). If Shrek is a RI domiciliary: complete diversity. The AIC > $75k.

## IPJ: If Case Brought in NH

Donkey is a NH domiciliary, so the NH federal court has general jurisdiction.

For Shrek more complicated. NH's long-arm statute goes to the limits of the Constitution; the federal court will piggyback, 4(k)(1)(a).

Because the video featuring Shrek and forming the basis for the claim was taken in NH, and perhaps uploaded there, there is likely minimal connection between the forum, theory of relief, and Shrek. Shrek had to enter New Hampshire to film the video with a New Hampshire resident. As discussed below, the harm likely occurred in MA, where the MA stock lost its value, so that provides only a weak connection between Shrek and forum. Shrek might have businesses in NH; however, MM is suing Shrek himself, not his businesses.

Jurisdiction in NH would be arguably fair and reasonable. NH has interest in governing misconduct that allegedly occurred in that state. It is convenient and efficient for all parties — evidence of the deepfake video (including Elsa Anna's testimony) is likely in NH. NH also has an interest in protecting its citizen (Donkey), and if the court determines that the injury occurred in MA (NH 1st-restatement), Donkey will be potentially protected in NH court because MA substantive law allows Public Access defense.

## If the Case Brought in MA

<!-- page: 2 -->

Shrek will either be considered actually at home (if a MA domiciliary) or likely essentially "at home" (lives five nights a week and pays taxes there, conducts his business there, plus the MA long-arm to the limits of the Constitution together with 4(k)(1)(A) completes the "at home").

The MA Court's exercise of IPJ over Donkey is more difficult. Donkey isn't domiciled/essentially "at home" in MA, so only specific IPJ is possible. We have the MA's long-arm and 4(k)(1)(a).

Regarding minimal contacts, Donkey ships 25% of his gum directly into MA (not through an intermediary, as in *Nicastro*), and the gum was the subject matter of the tortious conduct. The tort arguably concerned the MA activities of Mulan/MM, whose reputation is entirely located in MA (she only makes videos about MA locations). The prompt says the harm occurs where the business lost its value, in MA, as Donkey knew and (allegedly) intended. The arguments against IPJ over Donkey are that he maintains no offices there, has no agent there, and solicits no business there (or anywhere else); his business is "passive" in that he fulfills orders that he receives from the Internet, and as far as one can tell, has never visited the state.

## Q2

If MM sues in NH, a 1st restatement state, we must determine where "blood spilled." The prompt specifies that the last event necessary for the tort to blossom into existence is the loss of value to the business, which almost certainly occurred in MA, where MM is incorporated and where Mulan, its sole shareholder, resides and has her reputation. Under the 1st restatement, all major substantive issues are determined by MA, so the PA defense is available.

If MM sues in MA, 2nd restatement (blood-spilled + center of gravity/interests). The video was recorded in NH, but MM was harmed in MA, where the loss of value occurred and where its reputation is grounded (though it has viewers from everywhere, MM engages significantly with MA landmarks and public). Shrek's exchange with MM also occurred (to the extent it occurred anywhere) in MA, and MA has interest in protecting citizens and in governing the relationship between Mulan and Shrek; the availability of the PA defense relates more to this MA relationship than anything else. If MA court chooses MA substantive law, then the PA defense applies — more likely outcome. True, NH law means no PA defense, which if applied, ends up harming its own NH citizen (Donkey), and vice versa for MA law and MM. Overall, the defense is likely to apply because arguably most harm was felt in MA.

## 2020-assignment-01 · Exemplary student answer 3

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-01-nachmany-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

## SMJ

The location of the federal court does not matter for SMJ, so for both MA and NH federal courts: MM's suit asserts a state law cause of action, so no AUJ (*Mottley*). There may be DJ. AIC > $75K for each claim. MM is citizen of MA only (incorporated and PPoB in MA). Donkey is a NH domiciliary, so no DJ issue with him. Shrek may be either a RI or MA domiciliary. If he is a RI domiciliary, the federal court has DJ over both suits. If he is an MA domiciliary, MM/Shrek suit violates complete diversity (*Strawbridge*). Shrek is probably a MA domiciliary. The rule in *Mas* is: "you are where you are 'til you get where you're going." Shrek was a RI domiciliary through graduation from business school. Buying an apartment in MA, sleeping in MA during majority of the week, and paying taxes in MA, however, all point to MA domicile; voting in RI and living there on weekends point to RI domicile. The dispositive factor in favor of MA domiciliary is that a business, unlike schooling, is permanent occupation in MA. § 1367-SJ is no help if he is a MA domiciliary: this is a *claim* by a *plaintiff* (MM) against a *person* (Shrek) made a party under FRCP 20, whereby exercising SMJ would be inconsistent with § 1332 (incomplete diversity).

## PJ

PJ is different for NH and MA federal courts.

In NH, the court has general PJ over Donkey because it's his "real home court." The NH court may have specific jurisdiction, *International Shoe*, over Shrek. There is a long-arm statute going to the limits of the constitution, plus 4(k)(1)(a), so the only question is DPC. Assuming Elsa Anna is correct, Shrek has minimal contacts with NH by virtue of going there to transact business (the deepfake, the subject of the litigation) with Donkey. The exercise of PJ over Shrek is "fair and reasonable" — NH has an interest in stopping shady business in its state; at least some evidence is at Donkey's NH headquarters, plus Anna is likely there; it is convenient enough for Shrek, who traveled there at least once. One contrary factor is that the court will likely apply MA law.

In MA, the court has PJ over Shrek. Arguably Shrek is a MA domiciliary, but even if not, MA has a sufficiently broad long-arm statute, plus 4(k)(1)(a); Shrek has minimal contacts with the state through his business (which relates to this lawsuit), and the exercise of PJ is fair and reasonable because MA has an interest in preventing its businesses from being disparaged, MA is evidently a convenient location for Shrek, and MA law will likely apply. One contrary factor: most evidence is likely in NH. MA court probably has PJ over Donkey. He is a NH domiciliary, so the question is specific jurisdiction. The long-arm statute is sufficient, 4(k)(1)(a). Donkey arguably has minimal contacts — he sells the highest % of his products to MA (direct sales), and these products are related to the lawsuit. PJ fairness and reasonableness closer here — MA has an interest in its businesses and MA law will likely apply, but evidence is still in NH and MA is less convenient for Donkey.

## Public Access Defense

<!-- page: 2 -->

NH, an R1 state, applies *lex loci delicti* — "blood" was spilled in MA, because the *last* event (*Carroll*) is loss of business value. MM's business value is arguably "in the clouds," but the more likely interpretation is that as a MA corporation it lost value in MA. MA law applies.

MA, an R2 state, applies a presumption of *lex loci delicti*, but court considers 7 other factors. This is a substantive issue (regulates pre-lawsuit conduct, as lack of Public Access defense may make individuals less likely to disparage businesses). As above, "blood" was spilled in MA. There is no clear need for interstate system to apply non-MA law for a defense in MA about a MA business. Analyzing policies of forum vs. policies of other interested states, MA law should control with this affirmative defense, because MA's interest is greatest (how to protect a MA business). Justified expectations of MM were that being disparaged likely would not always result in recovery because of public access defense. The policies underlying the law: MA has chosen to promote free speech in this manner, which runs a risk MA was willing to tolerate of nasty conduct. Uniformity of result may cut either way: may not be uniform for resolution of business disparagement schemes originating in NH, but may be uniform for business disparagement against MA businesses (why should MM get to recover just because the scheme happened one state away, when other MA businesses might not get to recover if disparagement was planned and executed in-state?). Finally, MA court will have easier time applying MA law.

## 2020-assignment-01 · Exemplary student answer 4

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-01-okoye-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

## 1. SMJ through AUJ or DIV

- **Under § 1331:** MM can't establish AUJ because the tort-claim is a state-COA with no federal issue (so no ***Smith-Grable***).
- **Under § 1332:** The AIC is > $75k. Donkey is domiciled in NH, and MM is a citizen of MA (PPoB and incorporation). If Shrek is domiciled in RI, then we have complete diversity; if in MA then we don't (**Strawbridge**). Shrek was previously domiciled in RI, and still votes there, but is likely now in MA. He has taken up residence in MA, works there, and lives mostly there. Simultaneously, he pays taxes, evidence of intent to stay (**Mas**). The court should likely dismiss for lack of SMJ.

## 2. HCOL

- In NH, a R1 state, the substantive law of MA will likely apply. The last event necessary to complete the tort was when MM's business, which is based in MA, lost value and thus was harmed (lex loci delicti). MM is an MA corporation, with content entirely generated in MA, reinforcing that the harm occurred in MA. Because MA-law applies, public access defense will be available.
- In MA, an R2 state, the deference to lex loci delicti means MA likely applies MA law. Additionally, it would not be unfair to apply MA law. True, the video was filmed in NH, so it is likely that the center-of-gravity is NH. NH has an interest in governing illegal conduct within its state. But MA has an interest in governing conduct between its residents/businesses (Shrek and MM/Mulan). In particular, these points apply to the issue of whether the public-access-defense, an affirmative-defense turning on the MA-based relationship of Shrek and MM, is available (**Babcock**). While it is unclear if NH has any interest in regulating an MA-based relationship, MA has an interest as the business harmed here was in MA. MA also has an interest in regulating liability for this tort as evidenced by the existence of the affirmative defense.

## 3. Personal Jurisdiction

Per FRCP 4(k)(1)(a), federal courts piggyback on the long-arm statute of the state in which they sit; hence both federal courts can exercise IPJ where their state courts could. With long-arm statutes going to the limits of the constitution, the focus shifts to whether there are minimal contacts.

### IPJ over Shrek: Likely in MA, but not NH

- If Shrek is domiciled in MA, then an MA federal court would have general IPJ over him. If Shrek is domiciled in RI, MA could still have general IPJ under **Daimler** because Shrek is essentially "at home" there — he practically lives there, works there, and his contacts are continuous and systematic enough. If no general IPJ, we need specific IPJ (*International Shoe*). MA has specific IPJ over Shrek because Shrek has minimal contacts in MA. He purposely reached into and availed himself to MA by living there, paying taxes there, doing business there, and he probably wrote the emails that began the whole sequence of events

<!-- page: 2 -->

from his MA offices (*International Shoe*). It would be fair and reasonable to exercise IPJ over Shrek. MA would be a convenient forum because key evidence (loss of MM value) is in MA, and MA has an interest in protecting both MM and Shrek and in regulating conduct (by Shrek) that occurred partially in its borders. Also, MA would probably apply MA law, see above.

- In NH, there is no general IPJ over Shrek. NH probably also has no specific IPJ over Shrek. Although Shrek traveled into NH to commit the tort, most of his conduct occurred in MA, and the harm was felt there, making this single contact too tenuous (*Worldwide Volkswagen*). It would also be unfair and unreasonable to sue in NH. MA would be convenient as two of the parties already live there; most of the evidence is online hence readily available (although a key witness lives in NH, she could testify through an affidavit/video-conference/short visit to MA). MA has a stronger interest in regulating its residents/businesses (here, both Shrek and MM), and MA law likely applies.

### IPJ over Donkey: NH (yes), MA (probably)

- Donkey is domiciled in NH so NH has general IPJ over him.
- MA probably has no general IPJ over Donkey. His shipments are not systematic and continuous enough when compared to his business in other states to establish "at home" general IPJ (*Daimler*). But MA can likely exercise specific IPJ over Donkey. Donkey has reached into MA and has purposely availed himself to the privilege of doing business in MA. Under the effects test (*Luban*), Donkey targeted an MA resident, whose reputation is tied to MA, and MM felt the brunt of the tort in MA. It would be fair and reasonable to exercise IPJ. Though Donkey is a NH homebody, MA has stronger interests in litigating this issue (see above), and MA law likely applies.

## Substantive Assignment

Massachusetts is a Second Restatement state for choice of law. New Jersey and Rhode Island are First Restatement states. All three states have long-arm statutes that allow their courts to exercise jurisdiction whenever the U.S. Constitution would so permit.

Federal common law upholds so-called "choice of law clauses" in contracts, which are provisions in contracts specifying that disputes related to that contract will be governed by the law of a specified state.

The law in all fifty of the United States includes the tort of "Business Disparagement." Under this tort, a defendant is liable to a plaintiff if the defendant participates in the production of a false or misleading video that harms the reputation, public perception, or standing of a business in a way that causes a loss of value to the business. The last event necessary to complete the Business Disparagement tort is the infliction of the harm, in the form of the loss of value by the business or of the stock of that business. But: Massachusetts, unlike any other state in the United States, provides an affirmative defense to the Business Disparagement tort called the "Public Access" defense. Under Massachusetts law, the Public Access defense means that if a defendant proves that the affected business has constant and ready access to the public or to media outlets, then the plaintiff cannot recover.

In early 2019, the legislatures of Massachusetts and Rhode Island (but not New Jersey) passed the Uniform Forum Selection and Choice of Law Prohibition Act. Under this Act, which since early 2019 has been the law in both Massachusetts and Rhode Island, both choice of forum clauses and so-called "choice of law" clauses (clauses purporting to specify either the choice of law rules that will apply to a dispute or the substantive law that will apply to a dispute) are invalid, and may not be enforced in court.

Shrek was a business investor domiciled in Rhode Island with a net worth of approximately $150,000,000. Shrek commuted daily to Boston, MA to work in his offices there, but he returned home every night to his family in Rhode Island.

MM Corporation, Inc. was a New Jersey corporation with its principal place of business in Jersey City, New Jersey. Its stock was publicly traded on various stock markets. MM had various business ventures, one of which involved monetizing the cult of Mulan, one of its employees. In other words, Mulan performed stunts and video tricks in Massachusetts and posted the resulting videos online, hoping to drive traffic to MM social media accounts, which sold advertising. MM ran a website, a Facebook page, an Instagram account, a Twitter feed, and various other social media accounts and outlets. Mulan used these platforms to post photographs she took; short videos that she made of her encounters with people she met on the street, often while she was in disguise; and other short videos in which Mulan provided her opinion on current events and the quality of various products available on the market. All of the photographs and videos Mulan made featured recognizable landmarks in Boston or elsewhere in Massachusetts.

MM generated revenue by selling space for advertisements on its social media accounts. Those ads were exclusively for products that Mulan endorses on its social media accounts. MM never accepted an

<!-- page: 3 -->

ad solicitation until Mulan had first tested/used the product and decided whether she liked it. Because it sold no products of its own, MM depended entirely on the Internet traffic it drove to its social media accounts. As such, its reputation was essential to its business.

A key component of MM's business model was the public image and reputation that neither MM, nor Mulan, nor anyone else associated with MM ever accepted money to buy Mulan's endorsement. A banner on MM's website and its Facebook page said, "MM Is Your Independent Voice. Jeff Bezos Doesn't Have Enough Money To Buy Us." MM endorsed only products Mulan bought in Massachusetts (where she could easily obtain them), although those products might also be sold elsewhere. When MM endorses a product, Mulan posted videos of herself consuming or otherwise using it at a recognizable Massachusetts location.

One of the products MM endorsed in the fall of 2019 was Donkey's Best Blowing Gum. In November of 2019, Mulan posted a video of herself, in disguise, handing out free samples of Donkey's gum to kids on the Boston Common, then interviewing those kids to ask them what they thought. Donkey's gum was popular with kids aged 8–12. The video concluded with Mulan unmasking herself, to the delight of the children who had gathered for the free gum samples, and saying, "As you can see, Massachusetts loves you, Donkey's Best Blowing Gum. We give you five bubbles out of five!"

In December of 2019, Mulan received an email from Shrek. In the email, Shrek offered to invest in MM by buying a substantial number of shares of MM stock currently held by the company itself. Mulan forwarded the email to the appropriate company official, and negotiations proceeded quickly. All negotiations were conducted in Shrek's Boston offices. In early February of 2020, in Boston, Shrek and MM signed a contract. Pursuant to the contract, Shrek purchased $15,000,000 worth of MM stock and became a member of the MM board of directors. The contract included the following provision:

> Choice of forum and choice of law: Any and all lawsuits (including claims sounding in tort or breach of contract) relating in any way to the business relationship between Shrek and MM shall occur in either a state court in Massachusetts or in the United States District Court for the District of Massachusetts. In addition, in any such dispute, New Jersey substantive law will apply.[^1]

Around February 2 of 2020, Mulan received an email from a friend with a link to a YouTube video. The video was a short clip, about five minutes in length. It showed what purported to be a conversation in a conference room at the Springfield, MA offices of Donkey, the owner of the company that manufactured Donkey's Best Blowing Gum. The discussion was purportedly between Mulan and Donkey, and in it, Mulan purportedly proposed that Donkey pay Mulan $50,000 in exchange for Mulan's endorsement of Donkey's Best Blowing Gum on "all of MM's social media accounts." In fact, Mulan had never set foot in the town of Springfield, MA, much less in Donkey's offices. Mulan had never communicated in any way with Donkey, nor had she ever met Donkey in person. This video was a "deepfake," a highly realistic fake video. MM threatened to sue Donkey; Donkey quickly settled the dispute, and in doing so revealed two facts: (i) Shrek, conspiring with Donkey through secret meetings in Donkey's office in Springfield, had produced the deepfake video at Donkey's Springfield, MA offices, and (ii) Shrek had

[^1]: To clarify, this last sentence is a so-called "choice of law" clause in a contract. If a court rules that it governs in a lawsuit, this clause would mean that NJ substantive law would apply without the need for a choice of law analysis.

<!-- page: 4 -->

shorted a large amount of MM stock, so that he stood to gain a great deal of money if the MM stock price fell.

In fact, the stock price did fall. Some MM stock that MM itself owned became worth substantially less, resulting in a harm to MM of approximately $500,000. You may assume that for the purposes of this assignment that MM's stock, like the company, was located in New Jersey.

On June 2, 2020, MM sued Shrek in the United States District Court for the District of Rhode Island for $500,000. The complaint contained a single count, business disparagement against Shrek for the production of the deepfake video. Shrek moved to transfer the case to the District Court in Massachusetts, citing the forum selection clause.

## Assignment Questions

1. You are a clerk for the Rhode Island federal judge considering the case. Your judge asks you whether Shrek's motion to transfer should be granted. How do you respond?

Suppose that the Rhode Island court grants the motion, and the case is transferred to Massachusetts federal district court.

2. You are a clerk for the Massachusetts federal judge considering the case. Your judge asks you whether the Massachusetts Public Access defense will be available to Shrek in this lawsuit. How do you respond?

## 2020-assignment-02 · Exemplary student answer 1

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-02-ban-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

## 1. Motion to Transfer

SMJ is satisfied because defendant (Shrek; RI) and plaintiff (MM; NJ) are diverse; the AIC is $500,000 > $75,000. IPJ (RI long arm) and venue (§ 1391(b)(1) & (c)) are satisfied in D.R.I. because Shrek is domiciled in RI.

The facts of this case are analogous to *Stewart*.

Following *Stewart*, motions to transfer to comply with FSCs should be governed by federal statute and therefore any state law (MA or RI) that attempts to invalidate FSCs will not be applied, similarly to Alabama law. Therefore, *Stewart* points to the conclusion that the case should be governed by the federal transfer statutes, as interpreted in subsequent cases, particularly *Atlantic Marine*.

(*No Erie analysis here because* *Stewart* *is directly on point to the facts of our case.*)

According to *Atlantic Marine*, transfers should ordinarily be granted if plaintiff filed a suit in a forum other than the forum indicated in the FSC and defendant moved to transfer to the chosen forum. Private interests are not considered in light of the FSC and courts should only deny the transfer only when extraordinary public interest factors and circumstances unrelated to the convenience of the parties clearly disfavor a transfer.

Following *Atlantic Marine*, defendant's request to transfer to MA should be granted because there are no recognizable public interest factors present here that would clearly favor RI over MA.

Finally, *Hoffman* tells us that a case can only be transferred to a court where it could have been brought (SMJ, IPJ, VENUE). SMJ is satisfied for the same reasons in MA as in RI. For IPJ, MA has a long-arm statute going to the limits of the Constitution, and FRCP 4(k)(1)(A) makes it applicable in D.M.A. Minimal contacts can be established between defendant and MA. Defendant commuted daily to MA where his office was located. Defendant clearly enjoyed the benefits and protection of MA by conducting his business, including the business relevant to this lawsuit. It is both fair and reasonable to bring defendant into MA. Efficiency concerns point to MA (both parties, witness, and evidence available in MA) and MA has a strong interest in the behavior of two companies that principally do business in its own state. Venue is proper under § 1391(b)(2) since a substantial part of the events giving rise to the claim occurred in Boston: MM operates in MA, Donkey sells product in MA, deepfake video produced in MA, etc. SMJ, IPJ, and VENUE are all satisfied.

## 2. Choice of Law / Public Access Defense

Under *Atlantic Marine*, we ignore the choice of law rules of the transferor court. The choice of law rules of the indicated forum in the FSC applies.

MA is a 2nd Restatement choice of law state. Therefore, we consider the state interests and the center of gravity of the case by cutting cases into issues. The only issue here is the Public Access Defense. We start with the R1 presumption that NJ law governs because the injury, the loss of business value, occurred there. But the conduct causing the injury occurred in MA. Shrek is domiciled in RI and MM is incorporated in NJ but the two parties' principal place of business is MA. MM and Shrek signed the contract in MA. MA seems to be the key candidate state. Again,

<!-- page: 2 -->

efficiency concerns point to MA and MA has a strong interest in the behavior of two companies that principally do business in its own state. Therefore, MA law should be applied.

However, there is a COL clause in the contract which indicates that NJ substantive law should apply. An *Erie* analysis is required here because while MA law invalidates COL clauses, federal common law upholds COL clauses in contracts.

First, under *York*, we focus on the outcome. Applying state law will likely lead to different substantive law being applied, which will affect the outcome of the case. Apply **STATE LAW**.

Second, the Harlan test looks toward how the conflict will affect people's behavior. Knowing that COL clauses will be invalidated will most likely affect behavior of businesses — possibly some individuals. Apply **STATE LAW**.

Third, applying *Hanna* majority we first notice that the federal law at issue is Federal Common Law. Therefore, the question to ask is whether the twin aims of *Erie* are satisfied. COL is a significant issue that will lead to forum shopping and unfairness. Apply **STATE LAW**.

Thus, MA law invalidating COL clauses should be applied. MA has the "Public Access" (Affirmative Defense) and it is therefore available to defendant.

## 2020-assignment-02 · Exemplary student answer 2

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-02-klein-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

## I. Motion to Transfer

Does DRI have SMJ over the lawsuit? There is no AUJ since the case does not involve a federal issue. DRI has DJ only if CD and AIC are met. AIC is met ($500K > $75K), and CD is met — MM Corp. is a NJ resident (incorporated in NJ and PPOB in NJ), and Shrek is a RI resident.

Per *Hoffman*, a transferor court may transfer to another happy court (or a court to which all parties agree). Is DMA a happy court? DMA almost certainly has IPJ over Shrek through MA's long arm statute and 4k1a — Shrek participated in creating the deepfake in Springfield, MA, and all negotiations re the purchase of the stock were conducted in Boston, MA. This should qualify as minimal contacts. DMA's exercise of jurisdiction would be fair and reasonable since Shrek purposefully availed himself of the benefits of doing business in MA, and MA has an interest in regulating the conduct of business and torts committed within its state. Venue is also proper under § 1391(b)(2), since Shrek's participation in MA in creation of the deepfake is an event "giving rise to the claim."

Finally, consider the public interest factors under *Gilbert*. Per *Atlantic Marine*, there is a presumption in favor of a transfer pursuant to a FSC. Thus, the court should transfer to DMA absent compelling public interest factors to the contrary, since the FSC operationalizes the private interest. Public interest factors appear to point toward a transfer, because the central role that MA landmarks and citizens play in Mulan's product endorsements ("MA loves you…," etc.) suggests that MA has a strong state interest in the lawsuit. Additionally, as explained below, it is likely that MA law applies.

If DMA is a happy court, DRI should transfer despite RI's UFSCOLPA statute — per *Stewart*, the transfer statutes are valid and applicable procedural rules that supersede state law regarding forum selection clauses.

## II. Choice of Law Analysis

Per *Atlantic Marine*, when transferring pursuant to a FSC, use the COL rules of transferee state — in this case, MA. But the contract says to use NJ substantive law. Federal common law would uphold the contractual COL provision, while MA's UFSCOLPA would find the COL clause invalid. What do the *Erie* tests say?

(1) *York*. The difference between state and federal law in this case would likely be outcome-determinative, because the public access defense is likely available under a MA COL analysis, but is not available under NJ law, and because the public access defense likely would result in Shrek's prevailing in the lawsuit. Use state law.

(2) *Hanna* (both Scalia and Ginsburg versions). Use *Erie*'s twin aims for federal common law. Disregarding the state statute can easily lead to forum shopping, because out-of-state plaintiffs seeking to enforce a COL clause will file in federal court, while those seeking to avoid an unfavorable COL clause will file in state court. This also results in inequitable administration since out-of-state defendants sued in state court can remove to federal court to ensure the enforcement of a COL clause, while in-state defendants would not have the option. Use state law.

(3) Harlan in *Hanna*. The state statute regulates primary conduct by mandating that a state's COL laws be applied to lawsuits heard in the state, which ultimately effects which substantive law applies. It is safe to

<!-- page: 2 -->

assume that contractual COL provisions are drawn up by sophisticated parties whose conduct clearly contemplates the effect of COL rules on their exposure to litigation. Use state law.

All *Erie* tests point toward the use of state law. If state law applies, DMA should disregard the contractual COL provision and conduct a MA COL analysis. MA is a 2nd restatement state, so MA's public access defense is potentially available even if the place of wrong was not in MA. The analysis begins with a presumption in favor of NJ law because the injury (the drop in stock price) occurred in NJ. However, MA appears to have a strong state interest in this case because the tort affected the reputability of MA-centric product endorsements; because the parties' business relationship was centered in MA; and because the tort was actually committed in MA — even though the harm was felt in NJ. Thus, it appears that it would be reasonable for DMA to apply MA law and allow Shrek to invoke the public access defense.

However, if federal common law applies, use NJ substantive law. NJ law does not recognize a public access defense, so the defense would be unavailable to Shrek.

## 2020-assignment-02 · Exemplary student answer 3

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-02-lupin-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

## Q1

**SMJ:** The requirements for diversity jurisdiction are clearly met, as we have complete diversity with MM, a New Jersey domiciliary, on one side and Shrek, a Rhode Island domiciliary (he clearly lives here, not in Massachusetts) on the other, and $500,000 clearly exceeds the required $75,000 amount in controversy.

## Transfer Motion

**VCOL:** Per *Klaxon*, the Court would treat HCOL as substantive in diversity cases and so will apply Rhode Island procedural law banning the forum selection clause, unless trumped by the transfer statutes. This is the same fact pattern as *Stewart*: the federal transfer statutes say to honor forum selection clauses, conflicting with state law banning such clauses, so we apply the valid and applicable test:

*Validity*: Article III gives Congress the power to create the lower federal courts, so combined with the Necessary and Proper Clause, Congress clearly has the power to pass the power to enact the transfer statutes.

*Applicable*: The transfer statutes are clearly applicable.

Therefore, the federal transfer statutes apply. Now to apply them:

There is no *Hoffman* problem as Massachusetts can clearly exercise personal jurisdiction over Shrek because it has a long arm statute that the federal court can piggyback under rule 4(k)(1)(a), he commutes to work there every day, giving minimum contacts, and the exercise of jurisdiction is fair and reasonable given the location of the transaction. Under *Atlantic Marine*, the transfer statute analysis presumes that the case should be transferred to the location selected by the FSC, and the court should only consider the public interest factors, not the private interest factors, and see whether they overwhelm this presumption. They clearly do not here, as Rhode Island has no real interest in this dispute, while Massachusetts has substantial interests. Therefore, the court should transfer.

## Q2

Because there is a conflict between federal and state law, we have another VCOL problem.

**If FCL:** FCL upholds choice of law clause, so look to NJ substantive law, which has no public access defense.

**If State law:** *Atlantic Marine* specifies that if there is a forum selection clause, transferee forum law applies, regardless of whether transferor court was "happy."

<!-- page: 2 -->

Therefore, Massachusetts is the relevant state. Massachusetts does not enforce choice of law clauses, so we apply Massachusetts HCOL rules. It is a 2d Restatement state, so we start with *lex loci deliciti* but apply the § 6 principles. Everything about these events except for the harm took place in Massachusetts; all of the conduct took place there; Donkey and Mulan are exclusively based there, while Shrek conducts his business there; there were no justified expectations that other law would apply, and the relationship was clearly centered there. Massachusetts has an interest in protecting its citizens and governing business relationships centered there; etc. Therefore, we apply Massachusetts law, and the public access defense is available.

### *Erie* Tests

***York*:** If the public access defense is available, then the plaintiff cannot recover because MM's business is communicating with the public on social media, so it clearly has access to the public. Therefore, the choice between the two is likely to be outcome determinative, state law applies, and the public access defense is available.

**Harlan Concurrence:** Availability of public access defense likely affects primary conduct — if the defense is available, defendants will be more likely to utter business-disparagement-like statements when it would apply, so we apply state law, and the defense is available.

**Hanna Majority** (for FCL, Ginsburg and Scalia are identical): Federal common law is the source of the applicable federal law, so we look to twin aims of *Erie*: avoiding forum shopping and promoting equal protection before the law. If we follow federal law, we likely have a forum shopping problem because interstate disputes can obtain a different outcome by using federal law and parties will therefore forum shop. We also don't promote equal protection because like parties are treated differently. Therefore, we apply state law, and the defense is available.

**Summary:** All *Erie* tests point to the defense being available.

## 2020-assignment-02 · Exemplary student answer 4

*Past student exemplar (content/course/assignments/2020-assignment-02-sultan-model-answer.md): circulated by the instructor as among the best in the class. May inform writing and organization; never doctrinal authority, never cited to a student.*

<!-- page: 1 -->

## Question 1

Motion to transfer should probably be granted. Under *Hoffman*, RI district court can transfer to MA if MA is a happy court — proper venue and PJ. MA has venue under § 1391(b)(2) because a substantial part of the events giving rise to the claim occurred in MA (contract with MM established in MA, MM recorded in MA, Shrek conspired in MA, deepfake recorded in MA). MA has PJ — forum selection clause ("FSC") indicates Shrek's consent. MA likely has PJ even without FSC; MA district court can piggyback off MA state long-arm statute (4(k)(1)(a)), there are minimum contacts in MA (contract established in MA, Shrek conspired in MA) and suit is fair and reasonable (evidence of tort in MA, MA has interest in hearing claim, minimal burden to Shrek). Under *Atlantic Marine*, usually transfer to FSC forum — no presumption in favor of MM's choice, private interest factors weigh for forum because already privately agreed (also better able to view Donkey's offices which may have evidence and better able to compel Donkey to testify in MA), and public interest factors weigh for forum because MA has an interest in hearing this case of tort committed in MA that implicates MA citizens and MA sellers (MM clients). Note — all 3 states declare FSC invalid, but state law does not apply because *Stewart* held transfer motions, including those made to comply with FSCs, are governed by the transfer statutes (as interpreted by *Atlantic Marine*) which allow FSCs to be considered in transfers. SMJ also present via diversity jurisdiction — complete diversity (Shrek = RI domicile, MM = NJ PPOB) and $500k AIC (> $75k).

## Question 2

First, *Erie* problem because federal common law upholds choice of law ("COL") clauses and state law (of MA or RI, the only two who could possibly matter here) invalidates COL clauses.

*York* outcome determinative test favors state law. Under federal common law, COL clause is upheld (NJ law applies, no defense) whereas under state law COL clause is invalid and HCOL analysis would determine which state law to apply (apply the MA defense, see below). Outcome of suit will be different because Shrek can use MA defense to prevent MM's recovery because MM has access to public outlets. *Hanna* majority test favors state law. Use twin aims of *Erie* to determine whether federal common law is valid and applicable. Federal common law encourages forum shopping; parties will move states to bring diversity actions because they want the ability to specify and enforce COL in contracts, in evasion of state laws that invalidate these clauses. Federal common law also promotes inequitable administration of law because plaintiffs have different access to federal law depending on diversity. Ginsburg and Scalia don't differ in approaches to interpreting federal common law. Harlan's primary conduct test in *Hanna* also favors state law. If federal law is applied, individuals will likely change pre-litigation conduct. Individuals, aware that COL provisions will be upheld, will spend more time and money negotiating and drafting these provisions in every contract they enter into. Therefore, *Erie* analysis suggests state law invalidating COL clauses should apply over federal common law upholding COL clauses. If COL clause upheld, NJ law applies and Shrek can't use the MA defense to prevent MM's recovery.

Next, if state law applies and COL provision is invalid, a COL analysis must be done to determine which state's substantive law applies. Usually, the happy court rule articulated in *Ferens* applies for transfers. Under the happy court rule, RI law still applies after MA transfer if RI is a happy court (which it is — venue is proper and there is PJ as Shrek has RI domicile). However, under *Atlantic Marine*, the happy court rule is not used to determine COL when there is a FSC in order to

<!-- page: 2 -->

discourage gamesmanship and maintain expectations. With a FSC, we apply law of the transferee court (*Klaxon* — apply HCOL rules of state in which transferee federal court sits), which is MA. MA is a R2 state and will therefore conduct a factor-analysis to determine which substantive law to apply. This analysis will likely favor applying MA substantive law, including the defense, because the conduct causing the injury occurred in MA, the relationship between MM and Shrek was created in MA (contract established in MA), and MA likely has an interest in regulating torts that involve its citizens and its businesses (MM's clients). If the factor-analysis favors applying NJ law, the MA public access defense will not be available. However, the only factors favoring NJ law are that MM's loss of value was felt in NJ (lex loci delicti presumption) and MM is a NJ resident (states have an interest in protecting residents — only difference between *Babcock* and *Neumeier* was residency of victim). Therefore, it seems more likely that factor-analysis will weigh in favor of applying MA law.

## 2020-assignment-03 · Assignment prompt

Assignment 3 (graded)
Civil Procedure 2 & 7, 2020
Professor Jim Greiner

Due date/time: 9 am on Tuesday, Nov 10, 2020
Word limit: 825 words
